ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U1	Good afternoon, honourable Members of the Riigikogu. Good afternoon, Prime Minister and other members of the government. Let's start today's briefing. Please, let's do the presence check! 36 members of the Riigikogu registered as present, 65 are missing. The following members of government will attend today's Question Time: Prime Minister Andrus Ansip, Minister of the Environment Keit Pentus-Rosimannus and Minister of the Interior Ken-Marti Vaher. Ladies and gentlemen, we have nine issues registered today, which is why I can allow only two on-the-spot questions to be submitted in each case.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U2	Let's go! Please, Mr Simson, a question for Prime Minister Andrus Ansip.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U3	Prime Minister! Today the Riigikogu is facing a second reading of the budget for the coming year. And as you know, next year will be greatly affected by the increase in electricity prices. In addition to the increase in tariffs, Elering's network charges are expected to increase by 13%, and to the end user this means roughly a fifth increase in electricity prices. The government has the levers to ease this price increase, whether or not by reducing excise duties, but you do not use it. You are using three times less effective levers, which is the reduction of renewable energy charges, which is precisely what they are reducing. At the same time, higher VAT is collected in the budget and dividends are taken from Eesti Energia. Why are you opposed to reducing electricity excise duty to the European Union's minimum rate?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U4	I've answered that question several times from this speaker board. I remember when we introduced the electricity excise resulting from European Union regulations, we did not create a new tax for nothing, but instead replaced the air pollution charge, which was still at the price of electricity, with an electricity excise duty. The introduction of electric excise was mandatory anyway, but we think that this was also the right step. If we had continued to tax air pollution, we would only have taxed our producers. Knowing that these environmental standards, the social standards that we are following in the European Union, are not being complied with beyond the border, we would have been exposed to pollution anyway and our electricity would have been less competitive. Non-taxation is no alternative, as damage to the environment must be compensated, even in part. We know that the environmental charges and excise duty currently in force are far from compensating for all the damage caused to the environment. And, on the other hand, we need to pay the budget, we have to pay out the salaries of rescue officers, police officers, teachers, etc.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U5	Please, Mr Simson, the first point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U6	Prime Minister! A year ago, you received a mandate using, among other things, the slogan 'The euro does not raise the price'. Last year and this year Estonia has been the country with the fastest price increase in the euro zone. And next year all other goods will also increase due to the increase in electricity prices. Until now, nearly 100,000 Estonian children have received free school meals, but the support for school food has fallen behind the rise in food prices. Why are you opposed to increasing school food support at least by inflation? This would allow Estonian schoolchildren to have a healthier, warm school lunch.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U7	You are probably as well aware as I of how much the euro has affected the price increase and how much the increase in prices in Estonia has been affected by the increase in the price of energy and food on the world market. And this knowledge tells us all that the price of the euro has not increased. Similarly, the price of electricity rising from the new year has nothing to do with the euro and, in fact, very little to do with the opening up of the electricity market. The increase in electricity prices is primarily due to the obligation to purchase carbon dioxide emission quotas needed to produce electricity on the market. That's what increases the price. It is, of course, possible to increase the funding for school lunch, but money is always a question in such cases. Where does the money come from? On whose account? We should raise taxes, someone should cut their salary. I hope that the budget debate will be meaningful today, not just declarative. Perhaps you will also find some real sources to meet this very important objective of increasing school meals for students.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U8	Please, Mr Simson, the second point to be clarified.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U14	Please, colleague Marika Tuus-Laul!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U15	Mr Prime Minister! In the case of electricity prices, it is still very much for the state to influence the price of about 70% if we are talking about network charges, if we are talking about the renewable energy charge and, of course, the excise duty, which is five times higher than the minimum requirement of the European Union, as well as VAT at 20%. Many countries have gone down the path of differentiation in terms of VAT. Plus, you take out dividends all the time. It's like an extra tax, it's like an electric tax. ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U16	The argument that we don't want to do anything about it isn't competent. It is probably well known that in Estonia, after Bulgaria, the European Union countries have the cheapest price of electricity. To say that all of this is happening in the same way without the government doing anything about it is clearly unjust. In order for electricity prices to remain acceptable in the future, the government has planned a number of investments that, indeed, you have criticised very strongly. We intend to ensure that sufficient production capacity is created in Estonia so that we have electricity at all times and that it is also at affordable prices. However, I will answer your last question: the government is not planning any special measure to help pensioners, but all those who need help from the state will, of course, also help the state, particularly with income support.

Parliament has dealt with this as a supervisory body itself and has also carried out an in-depth report on it. In general, the following can be said for the conclusion of the Ida-Viru case. Service supervision is currently underway, with the aim, in addition to a specific case, to look more closely at the entire police information movement, as well as the functioning and supervision of internal controls. As a result of this work, we can hopefully also draw conclusions on governance arrangements, particularly as regards this sector-specific governance, i.e. the relationship between governance in the area of law enforcement and the whole precinct. As we know from the beginning of this year, the status of the prefectures changed. This was amended precisely because it would be possible to use more and more clearly the resources for substantive operational monitoring on the ground, releasing the management of the prefectures from the administrative obligations and responsibilities that have been previously assumed. This ten-month work, too, is certainly reflected in this report.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U50	Next is Mr Neeme Big's question to the Minister for the Environment, Keit Pentus-Rosimethus. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U51	Minister of the Environment! 1 March 2013 seems to be a remarkable day. There are 64 hunting areas in Estonia whose leases will end on 1 March 2013 and which have not been reorganized in this way, as the Hunting Act requires in 2003. For example, only three out of the 15 hunting areas of Läänemaa have been organized in accordance with the law in force, while 12 have been prepared for hunting ground arrangements, but no further procedure has been found in the Ministry. However, most of these 64 hunting areas are not known for their management plans. The current Hunting Act has provided ten years for the restructuring of hunting areas. Hence my question: why has this process stopped? Why hasn't it been completed?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U52	A good questioner, as you know, is in the final stages of the completion of the new Hunting Act. And when it comes to the new Hunting Act, this law could successfully compete in the area of world championship inclusion. This law is essentially a section-by-section discussion with different parties. There have been debates, compromises have been sought, and they have also been found. At the moment, the bill has been last coordinated by the Ministry of Justice. As regards the right to use hunting areas or within hunting areas, there is no intention to amend the law in this respect. No changes are planned either for the users of hunting areas or for the borders. The system is quite firmly established and, in my opinion, does not require any fundamental heel turns. It must be said that, in principle, no agreement has been found between landowners or hunters' representatives. So in this respect, the plan is to continue with the system that it has developed.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U53	Please, Mr Neeme Big, the first point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U54	However, there is a question of what will happen to those hunting areas that will not be reorganized before March 2013 in the 64 regions still operating under the 2001 regulation. The situation seems to be that hunting societies currently operating are losing their hunting rights. It will take time to establish a hunting area in accordance with both the current law and the new draft. Will a large number of Estonian yachts simply be uncovered for an indefinite period and hunting societies will not be eligible for hunting, or how?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U55	Please, Mr Neeme Big, the second point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U56	There's no reason to be scared like that. Once again, regions where users exist and where the borders of the region are also in place will continue to work as they have done so far. No one loses the right to hunt, and we have no interest in creating a relatively vague situation that would arise if we said that we were to start creating hunting areas from scratch. There is no need for this and the system will remain the way it has now developed for the borders and users of hunting areas. If hunting areas themselves agree, for example, to change borders, this possibility is entirely available under the new law, but this requires agreement between neighbouring hunting areas.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U57	However, I would also like to ask in this regard that, according to the law currently in force, the deadline for the reformulation of the right to use hunting areas is ten years and, as we know, the Ministry has prepared hunting plans for the 12 hunting areas of Läänemaa, for example, but some have been standing at this stage for a year and a half, they do not have the signature of a minister. Why have they stopped?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U58	Again: since the drafting of the new Hunting Act has been a very long process, thanks to its very thorough involvement, that has been much longer than any party would have wished, I have not considered it reasonable to proceed with the management plans you have mentioned until all parties have been assured that there will be no very abrupt changes to the new Law. Once again, there is an agreement that no very drastic changes will be made. The new draft Hunting Act is not in itself the ideal solution for landowners or hunters, in the sense that if the draft law had been written only by landowners, it would probably have become quite different, and even if the bill had been written only by hunters, it would have been quite different. ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U68	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Prime Minister! Two weeks ago, in this hall, you and I discussed the problems and concerns of the Enterprise Estonia. ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U69	I am still of the opinion that the management of the Enterprise Estonia has acted in good faith and based on its best knowledge to ensure that EU funds are used in a targeted way. In the current funding period, the Enterprise Development Foundation has decided to provide funding for almost 7500 projects. Yes, in certain cases, there is a suspicion that the rules in force have been violated, but, to my knowledge, there is no suspicion that funds have been used in any way for criminal purposes, for selfish purposes or for the purpose of embezzlement. There has been an error in the incentive effect of State aid, i.e. aid granted to projects for which the related activities had allegedly already started, i.e. a contract had been concluded for the procurement of some equipment and only then was the request for funding submitted to EAS. As far as I am aware, 58 projects have been re-examined and have found a number of such projects where there are reasonable grounds to claim that the incentive effect condition has not been breached because the pre-contractual agreement concluded has not been binding.

On 1 January next year, the electricity market will be fully open. So far, it has been served to consumers as an inevitability to join the European Union. However, studies show that in a large number of countries domestic consumers have still purchased electricity at regulated prices. The regulated price for household customers was used by 14 Member States as of 1 January 2012, while in Bulgaria, Estonia, the Netherlands, Croatia, Greece, Cyprus, Lithuania, Poland, Romania, Slovakia and Hungary this regulated price has been applied to all domestic consumers. Directive 209/72/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council concerns common rules for the internal market in electricity. Article 3 (7) of the Directive explicitly states that Member States shall take measures to ensure the necessary supply of electricity to vulnerable customers in order to address the issues of energy poverty identified, including in the wider context of poverty. This can be done through national energy plans, energy efficiency improvements and benefits offered by the social security system. While electricity markets are open in many countries, domestic consumers are considered vulnerable consumers in these countries, which is why prices are regulated.

Among other things, the zoo conducts thematic schooling to achieve its statutory objectives, organises study circles and supervises interest clubs, cooperates with other research and development institutions in large-scale research and development programmes and provides educational institutions with a professional learning and internship base. Thus, the zoo is located in the administrative territory of the city of Tallinn, but is an institution providing natural education of national importance. Tallinn Zoo performs the tasks assigned to zoos internationally in Estonia and participates in several international projects, including supporting animal exchange programmes with its rich collection. At the same time, the zoo cannot afford the investments it needs, as the low standard of living for people does not allow for an increase in the price of tickets without a reduction in the number of visitors. For a long time, therefore, the zoo has been waiting for the construction of a new nature school and conference centre along with a new entrance building on the side of the road of the Builders. Tallinn Zoo has built a new habitat for many residents in recent years, but so far many animals have to live under poor conditions. The little garden of polar bears and the living conditions are proof of its true existence. ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U211	Thank you very much, Mr Anvelt. No further questions. I now call on Mr Pomerants, colleague of the Chair of the Committee on Legal Affairs, to be the rapporteur. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U212	Good afternoon, Mr President-in-Office. Ladies and gentlemen, We will continue to discuss bill 291. For my part, I would like to assure you that I respect the profession of a police officer with great respect, but I will be very forward in saying that I have not reached the same conclusions as my colleague, Mr Anvelt. The Riigikogu Legal Affairs Committee discussed this draft amendment at its meeting on 5 November. We did so in such a way that we also invited the Minister for Justice and the Minister for the Interior to the debate on the draft in order to discuss at all the attitude and responses to the attacks against the police. As a result of the debate, we have essentially focused on two nodes, which have also been through this Chamber. One of them concerns the victim. We also had a discussion in the Commission, which is still the victim when a police officer is attacked. Is Estonia a victim or a specific police officer? If it is an Estonian state, is it Raivo Küüt or is it Ken-Marti Vaher or someone else? But there's no need to invent a bicycle here. Since you ladies and gentlemen have plenty of time, I have an executive edition of the Code of Criminal Procedure with over a thousand pages. But it is correct to stick to the draft debate and therefore I will only use a few extracts from it. Firstly, I mention § 37 in the Code of Criminal Procedure, as it can provide a very concrete answer. § 37 (1): the victim is a natural or legal person who has suffered directly from physical, material or moral harm caused by an unlawful act by a criminal offence or by an unsullied person. Second paragraph: the victim of a legal person participates in criminal proceedings through a member of his/her management board or body replacing him/her, who has all the rights and obligations of the victim, including the right to testify on behalf of the legal person. And the third paragraph states here that, unless otherwise provided in this Code, the victim shall be subject to the provisions of the witness in the course of proceedings. If we now come to the question of whether, in this case, in addition to this particular police officer, the state could also be at least morally injured, the comments have said that moral damage is considered to be physical and spiritual suffering and pain, loss of reputation and other such circumstances. It is debatable whether moral damage can also be caused to legal persons, and particularly this is questionable in the case of the State, but also in the case of local authorities and other legal entities governed by public law. Therefore, the definition of the victim should, first and foremost, stick to the traditional approach and not try to be highly innovative. Of course, there are possibilities for this, and if it is a police officer as a special victim, then we can discuss the penalty rates involved. After all, a representative of the state has been invaded. Now another issue that you have asked Mr Anvelt very much about. This concerns the settlement procedure. In general, there is the impression that the settlement procedure is some sort of thing being dealt with by a criminal, or suspect, and the prosecutor in some dark place: in the corner of a bar, they agree and leave without penalty. Ladies and gentlemen, you are mistaken! The settlement procedure is mentioned in § 239 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, which states that indeed, the court may, at the request of the accused and the prosecutor's office, settle the case in the settlement procedure. The court is always involved in the settlement procedure. He or she may return the agreement he or she has been born and demand that it be amended if he or she thinks that any false grounds have been used, he or she may at all not accept the agreement on the basis of legal restrictions. And there's always a chance that the arrangement procedure will grow into a general procedure. As has been rightly pointed out here, the agreement procedure is not an option if not all parties agree. This must be accepted by the accused, accepted by the victim, whoever it is in the particular case, and, of course, accepted by the prosecutor. The law sets out the cases in which the settlement procedure cannot be applied. To give one example, you may find it quite unacceptable that, with the consent of the victim, a settlement procedure between the accused and the state is possible in the case of rape. This act shall be punishable by one to five years imprisonment. And the reasons are very human and understandable. If it were a general procedure, it would mean that the victim would have to go through a long trial in all its uglyness. At the same time, the legislator has found that, when it comes to a minor, when it comes to several persons, when it comes to damage to health, rape is also considered a crime for which the state does not agree. Now, you guys have been talking here about maybe it doesn't matter if it's a regular domestic brawl somewhere in a bar or an assault on a cop or some other authority. I'm sure there's a difference. That is what the Penal Code means. In the case of a common case of fighting and beating, the paragraph that could be applied is § 121, which refers to physical abuse. It provides for a financial penalty or imprisonment of up to three years. But we will come again to Article 274. We really want to talk about Viljandi's case, but I think we need to take this issue very calmly. A few years ago, when the Ministry of Justice published data on crime, it became clear that Pärnu is the capital of Estonian beatings. Now we're talking about Viljandi. But the law certainly cannot be made based on the fact that this or this has happened in one city or another. This may well be the motive for some legislative changes. The same § 274 also covers cases where, for example, pushing may be involved, but we say that it is completely unreasonable to have an agreement procedure in this case and should use a general procedure. Is it? I hope I've been able to explain the arrangement procedure to you a little bit. With regard to our activities as legislator, it is likely that only recently a revision of § 239 (2) of the Code of Criminal Procedure, which entered into force on 1 September last year, took place. We have been discussing this issue, in which case the agreement procedure could be ruled out. If we come to the second part of the draft, i.e. if it is a person who has committed a deliberate crime once, then in the case of him, the negotiation procedure would consist only of two subjects. First, the qualifications of the crime. For example, it is rape that did not involve killing, and the person says yes, it was rape. Secondly, the extent and nature of the damage caused by the crime. And then, as far as the type of punishment and the degree of punishment are concerned, whether it should be jailed and, if so, how long, or perhaps if there was a financial penalty, that part would not be. If we look at the integrity of the law, what procedure do we have in the end, it would definitely require changes to the other sections as well. At present, people can be convicted in the settlement procedure. Punishment policy has increased in recent years and the proportion of real prison sentences has increased to 60% in 2012. The average length of imprisonment has been in 2012, when we are talking about § 274, the possible five years 9.4 months. But every case is unique. When we are talking about a financial penalty, the average penalty in 2012 has been EUR 600. The Commission has discussed this draft and recognised the need that it should certainly continue to address the issue of sanction rates. It has also been said repeatedly in this Chamber that a Commission has been convened in the Ministry of Justice to carry out an entire review of the Penal Code, including a priority review of this issue. That should be the solution. The Commission decided to put this draft on the agenda for the plenary session by 13 November. Everyone agreed on that. The Commission also decided to propose to the Chamber that this draft be rejected. It was supported by 5 and 3 members of the Commission, with no abstentions. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U213	Thank you very much. Let's get to the questions. First question please, Eiki Nestor!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U214	To understand the Commission's position better, I ask you this. The current law does not allow for a settlement procedure when it comes to senior state officials, prosecutors and judges, etc. Why?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U215	I guess I'm just going to say, out of my mind, that there are higher levels of sanctions here. But in most cases, it is an opportunity to agree. Why? Because they are of such seriousness and nature that it makes sense to choose a settlement procedure based on all parties involved, including the victim.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U272	Madam President, thank you very much. Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen,
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U273	Excuse me for a second, performer! Ladies and gentlemen, please be quiet.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U274	We have once again come to the end of the process of dealing with an important and very large bill. This time it is a draft law amending the State Pension Insurance Act and related to the amendment of other laws, which, according to the explanatory memorandum, aims to resolve the problems encountered in determining public pensions, benefits and benefits, as well as technical issues that do not entail any substantive changes. Of course, it is commendable that at last the legislation on pensions, benefits and benefits harmonises the use of the "Social Insurance Institute" instead of the names of the implementing agencies. Since 2004, pension institutions have been structural units of the Social Insurance Board, but until now the legislation uses names such as the Pension Board, the Pension Institution of the place of residence, the Regional Pension Board and the Social Insurance Board. The lack of common terminology has given rise to sufficient confusion among applicants and even situations have arisen where applicants cannot understand which administrative body they need to address. Therefore, this amendment is very welcome and will ensure a common understanding of which authority will resolve these kinds of issues. It is also to be welcomed that in the future, the Witnesses will be able to prove someone's pension status in the Committee on Pensions of the Social Insurance Institution, and the Witnesses will be interviewed in court. This change will make it possible to shorten the decision-making process for the pension applicant to grant a pension and, hopefully, to reduce the workload of the courts. The procedures for settling disputes are also harmonised and provision is made for all disputes to be settled by the Dispute Committee at the Social Insurance Board. The draft also regulates the transition from disability pension to early retirement if the person was already paid an early retirement pension before the disability pension was granted. If the invalidity pension ends before the person reaches the pensionable age, he or she shall continue to receive an early retirement pension without requiring a pension application from the day following that on which his or her incapacity for work ceases. The amendment to the Parental Benefits Act suspends the parental benefit for the parent during the period of deprivation of liberty. By the way, we were told in the Committee on Social Affairs that this initiative brought out the social workers in Harku Prison, which I find a little strange, but that was the case then. This amendment concerns persons who have been convicted by a court and who have been sentenced to actual deprivation of liberty and the parent is in a detention facility with the child. It is known that children can stay in detention facilities with their parents until they are four years old. The parental benefit is now suspended during the period of deprivation of liberty. In addition, the Parental Benefits Act will be amended so that the Social Insurance Board will publish the maximum amount of the benefit for one calendar month of the following calendar year on its website by 1 May of the current year at the latest. However, the methodology for calculating the upper limit is not changed, which means that the amount will continue to be confirmed by the Government of the Republic. The latter, in turn, leads to the conclusion that, despite the decrease in the number of births, there is no expectation of lowering the upper limit on parental benefit and of channelling the available resources into other family benefits. Looking at the family policy of the government coalition, the most pressing problem now is the too big gap between short-term and long-term family benefits. The most difficult period begins at the end of the parental benefit, when the cost of raising a child increases considerably, but the benefits become sharply lower.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U275	Would you like more time? Three more minutes, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U276	The current family benefit system supports the birth of children in particular. The development and upbringing of children should be supported not only by raising the upper limit on parental benefit, but also by creating new kindergarten places, restoring the support for ranches and warm food for all kindergarten and school children. Instead of the services needed to support children's upbringing, something else will be created under the flag of the reform of family allowances. In conclusion, this bill, as well as many other drafts of the Government of the Republic, could be described as an endless series of technical changes, which, unfortunately, does not improve the livelihood and quality of life of Estonian people. The draft does not guarantee an increase of 6.3% in the pension index for the elderly in Estonia, but, as of 1 April 2013, pensions will increase by an average of 5%. The difference is quite remarkable, almost one-and-a-half percent. The 5% pension increase is expected to give pensioners an average of 15 euros per month. This does not seem to be a very large amount in the light of the price increases in recent years. However, in view of the price increase that awaits us in the new year, this EUR 15 supplement will become rather ridiculous. Since January 2010, food and non-alcoholic beverages prices have risen by 20% and housing expenditure has increased by 21%. Next, 2013, in addition to the general increase in prices, Estonia will be hit hard by the increase in electricity prices. The Government of the Republic will only lay hands on it. However, almost two thirds of people's electricity bill is controlled by the state. If we add 13.3% increase in the price of Elering's network service fee to the increase in electricity prices, we will get about 20% increase in people's electricity bill from the new year. In the light of these increases, the 5% increase in pensions seems to be unacceptably low. In conclusion, Bill 248 is certainly necessary and important for solving the problems encountered in determining pensions, benefits and benefits, but in addition to such technical drafts, we expect the coalition to make substantial changes to the lives of Estonian people. We expect changes that will enable the elderly, children, single parents and all workers in Estonia to live without falling into poverty and ensure that the working-age population does not leave for a better life abroad. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U281	The next item is the third reading of the draft law amending the Law of Obligations Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. Let's open the negotiations. On behalf of the Group of the Centre Party, Mrs Tuus-Laul would like to speak. Please, eight minutes!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U282	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Good ambassadors! Before voting, I would like to remind everyone once again that it was originally the aim of transposing a directive from the European Commission that would better protect consumers. Then the bill came up before you, which was prepared as a result of the lobbying of the Estonian Banking Association and the Estonian Association of Liising Associations and which does not protect consumers, as was the original purpose, but rather lenders and banks. On the basis of this draft, banks will be able to cancel loans to families in default even more easily than they are now and can only assume how many people who are stuck will lose their homes or other assets secured by loans as a result. The explanatory memorandum to the draft law points out that, in practice, the clause governing the cancellation of the loan contract has provided an opportunity for different interpretations of the term "contributory payment', which, according to the Banking Association, prevents the termination of concluded contracts if the person has delayed payments or actually defaulted. So far, when a person in distress paid off the main part of the loan and delayed the payment of interest a little, the bank could not cancel the contract because the term could indeed be interpreted in various ways. However, the terms "payments' and "repayments' are now included in the common term "repayments', which includes both the principal amount, interest, interest and interest and the amount of other charges. "Other charges' include, for example, the charges for ancillary contracts arising out of a mandatory insurance contract or the reminders that are sent to people. If a person pays the principal amount of the loan and the interest on the loan but does not pay interest or other contractual fees three consecutive times or this reminder, the bank may cancel the contract. We believe that this is an anti-consumer change, although at first sight it seems to be merely a clarification of the term. And how can you still charge interest on interest? In practice, if a person defaults, he or she will be awarded a debt repayment contract. Under the new law, the repayment consists, as has already been said, of a basic amount plus interest and other amounts. In practice, however, a debt repayment contract is no longer a consumer credit agreement, since it does not provide the consumer with loans for economic or professional activities or as a result. And since the debt repayment agreement is not a credit agreement, it is not subject to any restrictions on the consumer credit agreement. Consequently, it is not prohibited to charge interest, interest and any other charges imposed in the consumer credit agreement to protect the consumer, on the amount owed which becomes the principal under the new contract. Today, not a day goes by when we do not hear of tragic cases where whole families are being evicted. We can see how people's debt will be multiplied by the demand for new contracts. We have only recently heard that one person who took the loan, for example, did not receive any more euros from Bigbank, his loan increased to seven times the debt, and the payment schedule was signed for decades. We see banks selling debt on the Internet, loan managers say that the problem is not ours and recommend families with these children to contact the social services. But a man in debt doesn't go to court. Still, let us remember who created this loan boom in his day! Who lured people into borrowing at all costs? Know who the banks are! In fact, people were lent irresponsibly, borrowed without assessing their solvency and were given debts to people who were not actually able to repay it. The same fate can befall people who have taken consumer credit. The number of debtors is high and it is known what is currently happening behind the door of debt counselors there is rushing, because people are in trouble. For those who are already struggling with the repayment of the loan, the debt will grow as a snowball. Now the restrictions will be lifted, in addition to the retroactive amendment of the law. It is really shameful how the government, the Ministry of Justice and the whole coalition have made this consumer protection directive anti-consumer. This has not been the aim of the European Commission. Finally, from a technical point of view, I would also like to say why such an amendment was introduced in the context of the directive. By the way, the draft law amending the Law of Obligations Act and the Advertising Act, which essentially sets out the points of the consumer credit agreement, is about to enter the Chamber of the Riigikogu. It could have been very well to put a change in the definition of refund in there, but for some reason it was rushed. The point is that practice has shown that when transposing directives, the opinion on the amendment of the law is not sought from different interest groups, in this case, for example the Bar Association or the Association of Lawyers, but also from consumer protection, which probably would not have supported such a change. A certain green path has been chosen so that changes to the law can be introduced without a greater sound so that it does not reach the consciousness of all. Once again, the Central Party Group cannot support this bill and will vote against its anti-consumer law-modification. We call on the other groups to do the same. We very much hope that our opposition partners, the Social Democrats, will support us in this endeavour; consumer protection should be a common thing for all of us. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U283	Thank you very much. There's no more calls. Let's get to the final vote. The vote on this draft shall not require verification of presence. Anybody want to take a break? Kadri Simson!

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U287	Today's fourth item is the third reading of draft law 257 on the amendment of traffic law, public health law and medicine law initiated by the Government of the Republic. Do the groups want to open negotiations? Please, Viktor Vassilyev as representative of the Central Party Group. Eight minutes.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U288	Madam President, thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, This law, which you are now going to decide on, is one of those laws that does not attract any attention. There was no discussion in the Committee on Social Affairs about this either. Apparently, everyone was told it was a technical thing, either the recipes are patched up in one place or another, which is the difference. When you read the title of the law, it looks a little strange: it is a draft law amending the law on traffic, the law on public health and the medicine law. What is the relationship between traffic and drugs? Do you have to take some pills before you drive or after you drive? The law itself is actually quite interesting and, by the way, it is worthwhile to go into it, really. It says that the role of the controller of the prescription centre will be transferred from the Ministry of Social Affairs to the Estonian Health Insurance Fund. It goes because the Health Insurance Fund, as stated in the bill, is really linked to prescriptions, the Ministry of Social Affairs is not. That's what I'm getting at, but what else does the Ministry of Social Affairs have to do with anything? This is a problem that was highlighted by the strike of health professionals. In fact, before the strike, health care workers searched for two years to negotiate with. Nobody wanted to! In the Republic of Estonia, there is no institution or legal entity responsible for managing health care. The Ministry of Social Affairs prepares draft legislation. What our esteemed Minister of Social Affairs will order from them, they will write together, good officials. The Health Board issues licenses, i.e. licences, checks how services are provided, but does not run the health system. The Health Insurance Fund distributes the money that we pay for social tax, but he doesn't run the system either. Actually, there's no system as such. There's a bunch of private service providers, there's no single system that's subject to anyone. Processing recipes was the last relic left by the Ministry of Social Affairs, and now it was eradicated. If it has already been decided that the Ministry of Social Affairs will wash its hands of health care, then there is nothing to be done about it. Then who's responsible? Probably the Health Insurance Fund. However, it is the responsibility of the Health Insurance Fund to distribute the money that we pay for the provision of healthcare. Now they're gonna give him a ministry job. Okay, well, nothing really bad's gonna happen at the Health Insurance Fund. They're like good officials at the Ministry of Social Affairs. They can handle prescriptions. The draft also specifies the obligation for the pharmacy service provider to enter the information on the prescription issued in paper form into the prescription centre without delay after receipt of the prescription. So far, it's been given three days. I don't know where we're going with this. Supposedly, officials who process prescription data will experience some confusion if these data are not immediately received on-line. I don't know if we have to worry about those officials who can't do their job, but in fact, this point makes the life of pharmacists quite difficult. A lot has been said about the land pharmacies disappearing. We're trying to invent some alternative options and start offering drugs somewhere in the store, for my sake, even at the Statoil gas station, but this option would mean that they, too, would have to enter all the recipes on-line right away into the prescription centre. In general, there is a tendency to harass an entrepreneur, and it is a small business. We are talking about the need for a liberal economy, we need freedom to conduct a business. We should therefore encourage the good people who, in their own country, by using domestic resources, want to set up and develop a company. Pharmacies are actually small businesses. If we impose any additional obligations on them, it means that they have to either get better tools or hire someone to work, give someone additional tasks, these are excessive costs that they have to bear in order to make it more comfortable for the prescription centre to work. Where do they get this resource? Nobody gives money to pharmacists. They are not budgetary institutions, they have to earn their money and they earn it by selling us medicines. The only income they get is the money we pay from our own pockets for drugs. And, of course, when there's extra costs, who else than the buyer of the medicines is gonna pay for them? Now, of course, there are digital recipes. I started medical work 35 years ago, and then as a young doctor, I could take out any piece of paper and write a recipe, misce, da signa, fiat, numero... They're like Latin spells, right, which means take one substance and another, mix it together to get 20 powders that have to be taken in both ways. And there was nothing broken, and they were able to keep count very nicely! The recipes were very complicated, because then the powders and tinctures were used, which had to be mixed on site at the pharmacy. The pharmacy economy is, by the way, a very precise economy; the pharmacist is never wrong; he must never afford it; neither does his professional ethics. The apothecary is the one who is a special person who takes on this hard job, and that's what he thinks. You must, despite all the legal changes that are rolled at his feet, make him stumble. But he doesn't stumble, he doesn't fall because a good pharmacist has seen all kinds of times, all kinds of laws. He'll be fine! My time is running out. I wanted to talk more about how it would change the traffic management, that the data would have to be transferred to the Road Administration. The Road Administration is not ready for this because health data are covered by data protection and there are special systems through which this information must move. I don't know what's gonna happen to it. In fact, nothing bad will happen if this law is passed, because there is also a point that certain requirements are postponed, they do not have to be met immediately. The pharmacists will manage, and the Road Administration will manage, because they have really been given time to prepare for the transmission of databases. We'll get ready to vote, too. I believe we can all vote in favour. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U293	Our fifth item today is the second reading of the draft decision of the Riigikogu "Increasing the participation of the Republic of Estonia in the European Investment Bank" submitted by the Government of the Republic. I would like a presentation by Peter Laurson, member of the Chair of the Monetary Commission.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U294	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The Committee on Finance discussed the draft decision "Enlargement of the Republic of Estonia's participation in the European Investment Bank" after its first reading at its meeting on 5 November. The purpose of the draft is to approve the proposal to increase the participation of the Republic of Estonia in the share capital of the Bank and to authorise the Government of the Republic to carry out the activities necessary to increase the participation. The aim of the bank's capital increase is to support sustainable growth and job creation in the European Union. One amendment was received by Riigikogu member Rainer Vakra by the deadline for submitting amendments, 1 November at 18 o'clock. The content of the proposal was to take the necessary steps to increase the bank's capital at a slower pace. The amendment was not supported, as the rapid increase in capital will have a very positive impact on growth and job creation in the countries of the European Union, and accelerated capital payments will be important for ensuring a high rating of the European Investment Bank. The Commission also stated that this was a financing transaction and not a cost of the state budget, so the contribution of capital does not affect the balance of the state budget. The need for accelerated contributions was discussed in depth. The approach of other countries is the same: capital contributions are made as soon as possible. The point is, I repeat, promoting growth, creating jobs at a faster pace and maintaining the AAA rating. The question was also raised as to whether there would be any additional conditions for later payments. There are no additional conditions, the loan will still be granted according to the criteria set by the bank. The Commission unanimously decided to refer the draft to second reading on 14 November, to close the second reading of the draft and to put it to the final vote.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U295	Thank you very much. We're going to the questions. Please, Mikhail Stalnukhin!

The European Investment Bank borrows money from capital markets and provides low-interest loans for projects that improve infrastructure, energy security or environmental standards in the European Union, its neighbouring countries and developing countries. In addition, the investment bank does not use the budgetary resources of the European Union, the money is borrowed on preferential terms for projects compatible with the objectives of the European Union's policy. Around 90% of the loans are granted to programmes and projects within the European Union. In total, the loan has six priority objectives, as set out in the Bank's business plan. Firstly, cohesion and convergence; secondly, support for small and medium-sized enterprises; thirdly, environmental sustainability; fourthly, implementation of the "Initiation and Innovation 2010"; fifthly, the development of trans-European transport and energy networks, which I think include loans that were granted by Muuga Sadam and AS Elering; and sixthly, sustainable, competitive and secure energy, which I think led to the loan of Eesti Energia. Now we have been asked to increase our participation by an amount such as EUR 7138000. In addition, the entire amount should be paid. For its part, the state transfers EUR 9 million to the mandatory funded pension funds for residents. The paternity leave lost during the recession will be restored. In addition, the Minister for Social Affairs mentioned that needs-based child benefits would be added. According to the agreement, child support for families with one child living in relative poverty will be increased from EUR 19,18 to EUR 28,77 in July and from EUR 38,36 in 2015 onwards. The need-based support for families with two children will be €57.54 from the coming year and €76.72 per month from 2015. Existing family benefits will be maintained. In addition, the third and more children's allowance for all families with three or more children will increase in the coming year from EUR 57.54 to EUR 76.72 and from 2015 to EUR 95.9. Additional child benefits are not taken into account in the payment of income support. If three or more children are born into the family, the birth allowance per child shall be increased to more than three times the amount of EUR 1000 per child. The Minister of Defence stated in the Committee on Monetary Affairs that, like last year, the defence budget will remain at 2% of GDP in 2013. As a NATO border country, Estonia must have the primary independent defence capacity to ensure the Estonian state's survival and protection against any unexpected events. The allocation of 2% of GDP to defence costs ensures a stable and long-term development of Estonia's defence capabilities. The infrastructure investment budget will increase by nearly 27%. A large part of this money, namely EUR 9 million, is intended to improve the living conditions of conscripts. The central polygon of the Defence Forces will also be developed, the technical park of the city of Tapa will be built, and modern infrastructure will be built for the battle school in Võru. The biggest procurements will come next year, according to the Minister of Defence in the field of communication and IT. He also mentioned that in 2008 an Estonian national defence development plan was carried out, which was reviewed every four years. He stated that there was a highlight in 2008. The proposal has been drawn up on the basis of additional information from ministries and specific explanations have been added after each amendment. Amendments to the State Budget Act are regulated by the Basic State Budget Act and the Rules of Procedure and Homes Act of the Riigikogu. The sources of cover provided for in the proposal must be both acceptable and possible. ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U397	Please, Mr Enn Frontland!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U398	Thank you, good Chairman. Dear waitress, While waiting for this question and your answer, I will ask you about another subject. As we well know, everything is connected today. As a previous point, we discussed increasing the participation of the Republic of Estonia in the European Investment Bank in the grand hall of the Riigikogu. We asked the rapporteur, who was a member of the Committee on Monetary Affairs, how much this will affect Estonia's budget for next year. To say the least, he didn't answer us. Would the chairman of the committee explain this in more detail?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U399	Thank you, good Enn! Of course I'll answer where I can get away! Well, I guess I'll just have to answer. I'll be honest with you, I won't. I'll explain why it doesn't work. This is a classic transaction in the meaning of Estonian state money, where money is put into increasing a specific participation. Similar situations are not only in the European Investment Bank, which you have now asked about, but also in the IMF, the Nordic Investment Bank and the ESM, for example. These financial instruments shall be included in a specific share or share capital corresponding to the size of the own contribution. In essence, the situation changes that your asset, which may have been in the reserve or on the current account, is actually the size of your participation in a particular firm. It does not affect your position either positively or negatively. The situation would change if such an organization were to fail. In that case, it is likely that some part of the capital will be written off. There may be another situation: You are doing well because your capital, which you have invested in, will be of benefit to you. Such situations have occurred quite several times in several international organisations. In such situations, the growth that has arisen over time is generally not taken out or distributed between countries, but is further converted into subordinated capital, the holding remains the same, but the so-called capital injection increases. There have been situations like this in the past. In general, these organisations, mainly those I mentioned earlier, have been pleasantly capitalised in this sense. They have also, over time, brought revenue that has not generally been their objective. Neither the IMF nor other such organisations always have the objective of profit. But in answer to the question, yes, it does not affect our balance sheet in this context, in the sense of the state budget. I hope I answered you.

Consequently, a compromise has been reached and compliance with this electoral promise has been a little further.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U512	Thank you, Mr President. Chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs! The Unemployment Insurance Fund reserve fund was consolidated with the Treasury. ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U513	Thank you, my good colleague. Perhaps you were not in the Chamber of the Riigikogu at the time, but in fact this debate took place more than once. Last year, this topic was discussed quite thoroughly. I'll remind you of that. Last year there was a longer discussion on whether the unemployment fund's reserves should be separate, as they were, or whether they could be consolidated in the context of the state budget. There were quite a number of reasons, since it was much easier, cheaper and more practical to invest these reserves in various securities. Then the risks of fund managers investing, for example, in a specific security in an unemployment fund reserve will disappear and, at the same time, Treasury specialists will also invest in a specific security that could increase risks considerably more than might be feasible. Quite a number of reasons have shown that it is practical to consolidate the reserve of the unemployment fund with the Treasury. There were very clear guidelines and agreements between the management of the unemployment fund and the Minister of Finance. I remember there was a great fear that the unemployment fund would no longer be able to use its financial resources. These fears were removed not only during the debates in this Chamber, but also through the written agreements concluded later on. I would also like to say that if you look at the state budget for next year, you will see that unemployment insurance will fall by 1.2%, from 4.2% to 3%. It's very, very positive.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U569	Heimar Lenk, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U570	Thank you. Honourable performer! I come back to the elderly, i.e. pensioners. The pensions could have been raised by 6.5%, but you only gave the money 5%. How did this thing come to be decided that the pension increase was so weak, also considering that no pensions were raised in the year before last?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U571	Thank you. That's a good question. I was waiting for this question to explain why pensions would rise by 5%, not less, but not more. Today, a journalist wanted to know the same thing. I said it would be very difficult to explain in ten seconds, which is usually given in response to television. I'm trying now. Here I have the opportunity to explain a little more and more. I'm trying to explain it to you for the sake of this verbatim, and I'm trying to explain it to you when I answer you. Let's start by indexing pensions on April 1. In 2013, the pension index resulting from social tax receipts and inflation could in itself be 1.067 if I was wrong, or 1.063, in short, slightly more than 1.060. One could say that the pension increase could also be more than 6%. Why is it 5%? It should be taken as a basis for indexing public pensions on 1 April. If the index is less than 1, the law states this and there is a general agreement that pensions will not be reduced. So if this coefficient is 0.9 with something, then in any case the coefficient is 1, and the pensions are not reduced. This was the case, for example, in 2010 and 2011 when the coefficient was below 1. In 2010 the calculated index was 0,909 and in 2011 was 0,957, but the pensions did not decrease at that time. As the law states that pensions will not decrease if the index is less than 1, there will be a situation where the Government of the Republic is obliged to set off pensions without increasing or reducing them. I'm sorry! The government can reduce pensions. In a situation where the coefficient was less than 1, he did not reduce pensions but left them at the same level. If the coefficient is more than 1, the Government of the Republic will amortize, or offset, pensions within five years. He could have raised pensions by 1% at the moment, for example, and so would have achieved a faster set-off. He would have had that chance. But the government is based on both the consumer price index and the pension level of previous years, when the coefficient was less than 1. It will seek to address this as a compromise and cooperation in the coming years. This is what caused the situation where, with a 5% increase in pensions, we cover the value of the pension index in previous years, which was less than 1.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U585	Thank you, Honourable Vice-Chairman of the Riigikogu. Chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs. My question concerns the compatibility of next year's state budget or, it seems to me, rather a contradiction with the Constitution of the Republic of Estonia. On my desk is a letter from the Association of Estonian Rural Governments and the Association of Estonian Cities, which has been sent to the Committee on Monetary Affairs. They have pointed out in quite a number of points and paragraphs that they consider that the draft state budget does not comply with the Constitution. They refer, on the one hand, to § 157 and § 3 of the Constitution and, on the other hand, to the drawing up of a wider budget and, at the same time, to a number of lines concerning local authorities. In the past, the Estonian Supreme Court has indicated that several previous national budgets may have been in conflict with the Constitution of the Republic of Estonia. What do you respond to several hundred Estonian local government leaders and representatives of several Estonian cities and rural municipalities who have signed this letter?
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U586	Thank you. I think this is an important question. I understand that the issue is mainly driven by the fact that the revenue and expenditure of local authorities has been transferred into the state budget itself in the context of the 2013 state budget. Such a letter has come to both the Committee on Monetary Affairs and the Ministry of Finance. If you have noticed, I have not constantly quoted any passages, but I have generally looked you in the eye, but let me now read to you to some extent for the sake of the verbatim In particular, income tax and land tax are public taxes, regardless of the fact that they are fully or partially collected into the budget of the local government unit. The rightful party to the national tax, i.e. the tax authority, the administrator and the tax collector, can only be the State. The fact that the Tax Act provides that a public tax or part of it is to be received into the budget of a local government unit does not make the local government entity the recipient of the state tax. The law imposes on the State the obligation to collect tax on such a tax and to pass it on to the local authority. Despite the fact that according to the law the tax is collected into the budget of the local government unit, the payment of the state tax results in a legal relationship between the taxpayer and the State. As a tax collector, the tax authority collects funds received in the form of state taxes and, in the circumstances where the law provides for the receipt of state tax into the budget of the local government unit, it transmits them to the local government units in accordance with the legislation. There is a separate legal relationship between the State and the local government, which obliges the State to pass the tax on to the local government. Thus, in the case of state tax, where the law provides for the budget of the local government unit, two separate legal relationships can be legally distinguished: the legal relationship between the taxpayer and the State, the legal relationship between the collection of funds, and the legal relationship between the State and the local government unit, the legal relationship between the transfer of funds. In so doing, the State is responsible for collecting the tax resources within the legal relationship with the collection of the funds, as well as for transferring the collected resources to the local authorities in the context of the legal relationship with the transfer of the funds. As a summary of this, the alignment of the share of taxes on local government units with the principle of universality of the state budget does not interfere with the right of self-regulation of local government. What I told you is an understanding of the letter that brought to the table by the local authorities. If it requires a trial, I think that this dispute must be brought to an end.

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U634	Mr President-in-Office of the Council. Ladies and gentlemen, I have a little scrapper in front of me because I was going to talk about child support, but it looks like all the right and smart things have already been said by my colleagues. I'm trying somehow to put this thing so I can see the bell so I can't accidentally reprimand. I want to tell you (if this talk seems too numerical to you, then you can review it later in the verbatim) about how child poverty is assessed in other countries that we are trying to resemble, but which we are still not similar to when we adopt laws such as the so-called 'need-based' child benefit law. Indeed, I would like to introduce to you the criteria on which the UNICEF assesses child poverty. The total number of these criteria is 14. I would like to draw your attention to the fact that according to UNICEF's methodology, a child who has two or more negative responses to the so-called 'little form' is considered poor. Thus, the following criteria must be guaranteed for the child. First, three meals a day. Secondly, at least one meal a day containing meat, fish, chicken or vegetarian alternatives. Third, fresh fruit and vegetables every day. Fourthly, books of age and children's knowledge at home. The textbooks are not part of such books, since the textbooks are not meant for children to enjoy or play, but for learning or work. Fifthly, outdoor hobbies: bicycle, roller skates, etc. Sixth, regular hobby: swimming, playing musical instruments, etc. At this point, it would be fitting to recall that a few years, or even four years ago, if my memory did not deceive me during the crisis, it happened that support for children's sport somehow disappeared from the responsibilities of both local authorities and the state. The result is that children's sport in Estonia is no longer financed. So on this point, we look kind of ugly. Seventh, at least one indoor toy per child, including educational toys for young children: blocks, board games, computer games, etc. Eight, money to participate in paid school trips and events. Ninth, the child must have a quiet, large enough place with sufficient light to study at home. Tenth, the child must have a separate Internet connection. Eleventh, new clothes. This means that a child, even a third child in a family, must not have only old clothes left by his older siblings. He still has to have new things, not only bought from second hand. Twelveth, two pairs of matching shoes, including one pair, suitable for every weather. Thirteenth, the opportunity to invite friends over to play and eat from time to time. This is not a minor criterion. I believe that each of us has at least two or three familiar children who never invite our children or grandchildren to visit because they are ashamed to show their home, let alone eat. By fourteenth, the child must have the opportunity to celebrate festive events: birthdays, name days, religious events, etc. In view of all this (if any of you refer to the birds), I would remind you that two negative responses mean that the child is poor. In Estonia, for some reason, we think that the child is poor when his father earns 280 euros a month, his mother earns 140 euros a month and he retains 84 euros a month.
ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U635	And in that case, if the parents of that child go to the Social Welfare Department and take evidence that their income is just as high or a little less than that, they may receive EUR 9 59 cents. We expect this money to help our children out of poverty. That is a very interesting point of view. Finally, I will also talk about the results of the study. 29 countries participated in this UNICEF survey last year and Estonia is ranked 19. If we now take the trend of growth, Iceland will take the lead in the wealth of the children. There are 0.9% of poor children who do not have two of these criteria. The next one is Sweden - 1.3%, then Norway - 1.9%, Finland - 2.5%, Denmark - 2.6% and Holland - 2.7%. The eighth place is Luxembourg - 4,4%, then Ireland - 4.9%, Great Britain - 5.5%, Cyprus - 7% and Spain - 8.1%. The thirteenth is Slovenia, the fourteenth is Austria, the Czech Republic 8.8%, Germany 8.8%, Malta 8.9%, Belgium 9.1% and France 10.1%. Estonia is, I'm sorry, I accidentally lied to 20th place. We have 12.4% poor children. However, when we talk to experts, it becomes clear that we are relatively high because we have a compulsory e-school. This means that every child has no choice but to access the Internet. If it weren't for you, our score would fall. I would also like to draw your attention to the fact that in other countries where children are poorer than ours, such as Italy, Greece, Portugal, Hungary, Bulgaria and Romania, there is a slightly warmer climate. This means that if the child does not have, for example, warm clothes, it may not be fatal to his or her health. All this long talk is that I ask you, ladies and gentlemen, however, to support the Central Party's proposal to triple child support, regardless of whether the parents of our children live in relative, absolute or any other poverty, because in fact, we do not have very rich people in Estonia. Thank you!

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U644	Mr President-in-Office of the Council. Ladies and gentlemen, The State Forest Management Centre is the keeper, protector and manager of state-owned forests and other diverse and rich natural communities, who own nearly 40% of Estonian forests. Therefore, RMK is the largest forest owner in Estonia. As a profit-making state agency, it is one of the most important tasks of the state in managing forest income and ensuring the stability of the timber market as the largest forest owner. Equally important are RMK's activities, which do not directly generate economic income, but are even more important in managing our national resource, perhaps by creating free recreation facilities, performing the necessary maintenance activities in nature conservation, mapping heritage sites, supporting forestry education, promoting nature education, maintaining forest roads and drainage systems, etc. RMK's obligation is to ensure more forest regeneration and better quality compared to the current forest that is being cut. This means creating a new generation of forests and maintaining a young forest. Today, we can proudly regard ourselves as a forest nation, as 3,000 trees grow in our forests for every Estonian. Despite Estonia being one of the world's most forested countries, where the forest is covered by nearly half of our land area, or 2.2 million hectares, we should pay more attention to forest building and informed forest growth, i.e. forest cultivation and maintenance. In addition to many of the perks and wounds, the existence of which is certainly energy imperative, our forest industry needs sufficiently high-quality pine trees, spruces and lids. The topic of whether enough pine and spruce crops are being built in Estonia and whether they are being maintained enough to obtain high environmental and economic values in the future needs to be analysed separately. Nor must the education of society in forestry be neglected. The sustainability and quality of Estonian forestry must be assessed on the basis of the establishment and maintenance of young forests and not on the basis of cutting so mature forests. Against this background, the question arises as to why the State, under the direction of the Ministry of Finance, has started to undermine the competitiveness of the State Forest Management Centre. As a result of public policy, RMK has been excluded from the 2011 budget for ownership income and income tax of EUR 26 million, for EUR 45,8 million in 2012. The 2013 budget is initially planned at EUR 17.9 million. In total, it amounts to EUR 89.7 million, or more than EEK 1.4 billion. As a result of such manoeuvres, the government has taken the current account of the state company substantially close to zero. On the other hand, the 2013 draft state budget provides for a return to RMK of EUR 20 million, to be paid by 31 January 2014. Of course, the State will generate additional revenue from its interest rates. In short, the state cleans the account of the state company in order to maintain the budget balance, and with the other hand, RMK provides revolving loans for one year so that the company's economic activity does not stop, earning additional interest income on it. Yeah, well, the state can afford anything as owner. But can the situation be said about the prudent management of state property? "The manager of state assets is obliged to manage state assets in a purposeful, rational, sustainable and prudent manner," states § 8 (1) of the State Assets Act. "In carrying out any acts or transactions involving state assets, the manager of state assets must be guided by the principle of increasing the benefits and avoiding the losses which the State may incur from these acts or transactions," states §8 (4) of the State Assets Act. As of the end of 2010, the State Forest Management Centre used nearly 1.1 million hectares of land, of which 842000 hectares were covered by the forest. The rest was largely made up of swamps and bogs, and to a small extent other natural communities. 8115 kilometres of forest roads have been built to serve this vast area of land. As the forest land in Estonia is predominantly too wet, land improvement systems of 440000 hectares have been established in the state forest. In view of the average life span of the forest road of 20 years and the 30 years of the forest improvement system, approximately 400 kilometres of roads and 15000 hectares of drainage systems should be reconstructed each year. The vast majority of roads and drainage networks have been built between 1960 and 1980 or are even older, so most of them have now reached reconstruction age. Mostly due to the lack of money, but also due to the lack of design and construction capacity, the average annual renovation of 400 kilometres of forest roads and 5 000 hectares of land improvement systems has been made in recent decades. Although the pace of reconstruction and construction has increased in recent years, it is clear that without increasing volumes, the productivity of forest land will start to decline and the quantity of timber will decrease, and the revenue of the state will decrease accordingly. It is estimated that in the next 10 years, the failure to do the necessary forest improvement will reduce the cutting capacity of the state forest by one third, from the current three million cubic metres to two million. In the same rhythm, the economic performance of the State Forest Management Centre itself falls. In addition, the reduction in the added value of wood processing and its impact on the economy must be taken into account. The country should be more aware of the added value of wood processing. The state must not aim at collecting the maximum tax on the timber sales revenue of the forest owner into the state budget. Instead, the state should encourage the unhindered production of timber from mature forests and stands during the harvesting cycle. The main revenue for the Treasury must come through wood processing, sawing and furniture industries, transport, trade, etc. The State Forest Management Centre must have sufficient resources to build, grow, maintain and protect forests, as well as to maintain forest roads, maintain forest drainage networks, rest economy etc. Thank you!

ParlaMint-EE_2012-11-14_U648	Mr President of the Riigikogu. Ladies and gentlemen, Members of the Riigikogu! Why do I dare say "deception"? For teachers, Mr Jaak Aaviksoo, Minister for Education and Science, has promised that the minimum wage rates will rise by 11%, and in addition, every school will receive 20% extra money, for which it is possible to decide within the school who will be paid exactly more or less extra money. However, if we look at the figures in the 2013 state budget, it is clear that in reality, the increase in teachers' salaries is 4.7%. Next, we can also look at the Minister for Education and Research's signal that next year the minimum rates for teachers' pay will be raised to EUR 715 and a minimum threshold will be set. As a result, we can clearly conclude, which has now been understood by most teachers, that a junior teacher may indeed have a considerable wage increase, but it is not noted that there are very few teachers in Estonia who today may call themselves a junior teacher. In general, when a person has gone to school after university studies, usually in a year or two, he or she also reaches a teaching position. In the case of a teacher who is subject to a minimum teacher's salary and who is supposed to increase significantly in the following year, even 11%, but who is currently receiving, for example, an extra fee for the instruction of the circles, the management of the choir, or the work of the class manager, all the additional costs of education will be taken into account in the next year for the teachers' payroll fund. Really, there is no guarantee that he will, for example, be paid a grade manager's fee at all. Moreover, when looking at the decisions or proposals of the so-called Teacher Appreciation Commission, which have already been written into the new Basic School and Upper Secondary School Act in circulation, it has made it very clear to teachers that the bonuses currently foreseen for choir management, preparation for olympiads, repairing notebooks or dealing with children with special needs will be part of a single payroll fund. The teacher negotiates with his employer, or schoolmaster, and negotiates what his salary numbers and workload will be. Accordingly, for example, class management or recreational activities are actually included in their working hours. For example, this teacher, who is working today with a 22-hour load and also leading the class, can achieve a 20-hour load in the negotiations. Let's see what it's gonna look like in schools when there's no extra money and there's nowhere to come. It's the story that we're suddenly left with money in all the schools because of the drop in the number of children, in fact, the school people know a long time ago that this is not the case in reality. Teachers actually have two concerns. The first is that when class managers' fees are lost, it takes seriously the leading class that emerges in addition to the so-called burden. Social problems in school are increasingly acute. The second and very painful problem, which is covered by a major debate, is that if, next year, all education funds are to be transferred to the so-called payroll fund, i.e. the second part of the salary increase, all the other support services: logopaedics, school psychologists, social pedagogues, special needs teachers will suffer. With the current number of children, the percentage of children with special educational needs who need additional support for social reasons is increasing in our school. They may need support because their parents work very far from home or in another country, and they need support systems to continue at a normal textbook pace. If, in this context, it is unclear what will become of the whole support system from 2013, there is more than one reason for teachers' concern. What can we say in summary? If next year's budget stays with today's figures, the roller roller will roll on, then the disappointment of teachers will only grow. In a good case, we see teachers who express their disappointment with perhaps a strike or step out. Worst case, the teachers give up, leave school, and the question arises of whom we will see in school and class the day after tomorrow. Can there really be a situation where schools are closed not only because of the shortage of children but also because of the lack of teachers? In the second case, we are seeing more and more of these proposals, which, unfortunately, have already partly been made by the Minister of Education and Research, so that the school and class can come from people who have not graduated from higher education, gaining a teaching profession. They are otherwise nice people or, as has been said in kindergartens, they love children and are good to children, so they are also good to work in kindergartens. The next step, maybe in school? When we recalculate everything into money, we calculate that, for example, we do not have the opportunity to support our near-home school, there is no way to do one, the other or the third, there is a question that we want from our country at all. Let's hope these theories don't work and we're really starting to appreciate the teacher's work before the third reading. Thank you.
